# Rule 8 core: caller-owned workspaces and no host blocking in the graph API, FROST MoE GEMM and SDPA, grouped GEMM and Hopper KDA; R9 detector and recipes R10/R11

[Upstream PR](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181) · state: open · merged: False

## Original PR description

- [x] I reviewed the Hard Rules in the `AGENTS.md` for each directory this PR touches (see [root AGENTS.md § Reviewing a PR](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/develop/AGENTS.md#reviewing-a-pr-human-or-agent)) and my changes comply, or I explain the exception below.
- [x] I added GitHub labels: one `cat-*`, one or more `area:*` / `op:*`, and one `orig-*` (see [label list](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/labels)).

## Affected area

Python API or bindings; FE OSS kernels or CuTeDSL; CI or test infrastructure. Files: `python/cudnn/{api_base,tensor_adapter,__init__}.py`, `python/cudnn/AGENTS.md`, `gemm/frost/{sm100,sm120}/compiler.py`, `gemm/cutedsl/**` (grouped GEMM), `sdpa/fwd/api_dsl.py`, `linear_attention/hopper/**` (KDA sm90 engines), `test/python/fe_api/conftest.py` (R9 detector), the sdpa/frost, gemm/frost, grouped_gemm and linear_attention tests, `test/python/pytest.ini`.

## Summary

Rule 8 (#1167, mechanical batch #1168) applied to the code **our team owns**: the graph-API core, the FROST MoE GEMM plans, FROST SDPA, the grouped-GEMM CuTe-DSL APIs and the Hopper KDA engines. This PR supersedes the stacked #1171 / #1172 / #1173 / #1174, which mixed these with contributor-owned modules; those modules now come as separate PRs stacked on this one (DSA, NSA/CSA, flex_attention, HSTU, gated_attention_block), each reviewed by its owners.

- **Core.** `WorkspaceCarver` / `ws_align` / `_WS_ALIGN` live in `api_base.py` (re-exported by `sdpa/fwd/api_dsl.py`); `tensor_adapter.allocate_byte_workspace` is deleted (every caller carves from the caller's workspace); `torch_stream_context(stream, device)` takes the operand device.
- **R9 detector** (`test/python/fe_api/conftest.py`): every `APIBase.execute` runs under `torch.cuda.set_sync_debug_mode("error")` (per-class wrap once, `APIBase.__init_subclass__` hook for late-imported classes, armed per test; marker `allow_host_sync` for the declared R6 debug gates); `compile_allocates_nothing` fixture (R11). `test_rule8_detector.py` proves the detector fires.
- **FROST MoE GEMM** (`gemm/frost/sm100+sm120/compiler.py`): the caller's workspace is carved before the reduction outputs are seeded, so a missing workspace raises with every buffer untouched; the block-scale class docstrings state the contract.
- **Grouped GEMM CuTe-DSL** (`gemm/cutedsl/**`): every APIBase class gains `workspace=` (required when `scratch_workspace_bytes() > 0`); `backend_utils.allocate_wrapper_workspace` allocates for the wrappers under the launch stream; wgrad pointer tables are built by the caller layer (`wgrad_expert_ptrs`, discrete wgrad requires `wgrad_ptrs`) instead of `torch.tensor(host_list, device=)` per execute; `to_host_list` is deleted and the device-value validation sits behind the `CUDNN_FE_GROUPED_GEMM_VALIDATE_DEVICE_VALUES` debug gate (R6). New `test_grouped_gemm_rule8.py` (+ `_workspace.py::ws(api)` helper).
- **FROST SDPA** (`sdpa/fwd/api_dsl.py`): no workspace-less standalone fallbacks; `_scratch_base` raises the R2 contract error on a missing buffer; the grandfathered `_dummy` slots are pinned by `test_sdpa_fwd_rule8_ratchet.py` (23) so no new dead-ABI zero buffers appear; `sdpa/frost` tests supply the workspace through a conftest shim.
- **Hopper KDA** (`linear_attention/hopper/**`): `KdaHopperPlan` no longer keeps twelve scratch tensors in a module-level dict for the process lifetime (46.5 MiB at T=2048/H=12) and `KdaHopperCudaPlan` fwd/bwd no longer allocate absent-state scratch, zero seeds and staging per execute: all declared by `get_workspace_size()` and carved from the caller's workspace, zero seeds `memset_zero_async`ed on the launch stream every execute; `marshal.py` dtype conversions are build-declared staging carves (`staging_ports`; the one named interim Rule 2 exception, see R5), an undeclared or padded mismatch raises `NotImplementedError` naming the port.
- **Recipes** (`python/cudnn/AGENTS.md`): R2/R3/R4/R5/R9 addenda, R10 (host-int envelopes), R11 (compile from fake tensors / split lazy launchers), R7 (per-device handle, locked re-stream).
- **Workspace lifetime (Codex P1).** `backend_utils.retain_workspace` guards the caller's workspace (CUDA memory on the plan's device, else `ValueError` before any pointer is taken) and keeps it alive across the asynchronous launch: torch buffers are `record_stream`ed on the launch stream; immutable-framework (JAX) buffers are held per API in a list, each fenced by a CUDA event recorded on its launch stream once its launch is enqueued and released only when that event has completed, so overlapping eager calls never free each other's scratch (holding only the latest buffer did). Unit-tested with a long kernel in flight.
- **develop #750 merged (Rubin MXFP8 MoE EP).** Its wgrad plans allocated a plan-owned descriptor workspace at compile and exposed `descriptor_workspace=` / `get_grouped_gemm_wgrad_workspace_size_sm100`. After the merge the plans stay allocation-free: `descriptor_workspace=` is a keyword-only alias of `workspace` on the wgrad wrapper and the block-scaled class (torch block-scaled only, develop's validation messages kept; both given and different → `ValueError`), `get_grouped_gemm_wgrad_workspace_size_sm100()` returns exactly `scratch_workspace_bytes()` (asserted for dense and discrete), and the wrapper allocates per call when neither is given. develop's plan-cache isolation by dense-output address (`explicit_dense_output_identity`) is removed: it existed only to isolate the plan-owned mutable workspace, and keying the compile cache on an output pointer costs one JIT compile per fresh output allocation; develop's tests are adapted (one compile per signature).
- **R9 detectors** assert on the monotonic allocation counter (`memory_stats()["allocation.all.allocated"]`), not on `memory_allocated()` equality, which also moves when Python frees an earlier test's tensors mid-test.

## Why

Rule 8: a plan or engine owns no device memory and never blocks the host at build or execute. GC-timed frees of plan-owned buffers are the #1151 class of CUDA-graph capture invalidation; per-execute allocations make `execute()` uncapturable and slow under load. The detector makes the rule enforceable instead of a review convention.

## Related issues

#1167, #1168 (merged), supersedes #1171 / #1172 / #1173 / #1174 (closed in favour of this split).

## API and compatibility impact

**Torch-op layer (wrappers, `cudnn.torch`, `cudnn.jax`, `linear_attention.ops`): no signature change.** `test/python/api_index/api_index.txt`: no additions or removals from this PR.

Plan-level (APIBase class) contracts, which this project treats as implementation details of the torch-op layer:
- Grouped GEMM classes: `execute(..., workspace=)` required when `scratch_workspace_bytes() > 0`; discrete wgrad requires `wgrad_ptrs`; `tensor_adapter.allocate_byte_workspace` removed (not a public export).
- `SdpaFwdDslSm100.execute`: `workspace` required when `scratch_workspace_bytes() > 0` (the `sdpa_fwd` wrapper allocates it).
- Hopper KDA graphs: `graph.get_workspace_size()` is no longer 0 for sm90 KDA; `execute()` without a workspace raises the Workspace contract error (`kimi_delta_attention` already sizes from it); an undeclared padded input raises `NotImplementedError`.
- FROST MoE compiled plans called directly need `workspace=` (already so on develop; this PR only moves the check ahead of the reduction-output seeding).
- `kimi_delta_attention` (torch op) accepts non-contiguous q/k/v/g/beta as before (it repacks them in the op layer); a `cudnn.pygraph` caller of the sm90 KDA engines must pass a sized workspace (`get_workspace_size()` is no longer 0; `execute(pack, workspace=None)` raises) and an operand padded beyond what the graph declared raises `NotImplementedError` naming the port instead of being repacked inside the plan.
- `WorkspaceCarver` (adapters): a non-contiguous workspace is refused (develop's `reshape(-1)` copied it silently); THD descriptor carves require a 128-byte-aligned base (`align=_WS_ALIGN`), everything else stays at 16.
- `SdpaFwdDslSm80.execute`: `rope_freqs=` became `rope_cs=` (a prebuilt cos/sin table the wrapper builds with `_sm80_rope_table`); the `sdpa_fwd_wrapper_sm80` signature is unchanged. SM120 THD `get_workspace_size()` grows by one `v_stub` chunk.
- Grouped GEMM: the host-side validation of `padded_offsets` / pointer-table VALUES (a D2H read per call) is now behind `CUDNN_FE_GROUPED_GEMM_VALIDATE_DEVICE_VALUES=1` (R6); bad device values reach the kernel unless the gate is on. Removed module functions: `tensor_adapter.allocate_byte_workspace`, `tensor_adapter.to_host_list`, `tensor_adapter.get_version` (not in the public API index; no in-tree or known downstream callers). New public helper `cudnn.wgrad_expert_ptrs`.

Wrapper-compatibility audit (every `*_wrapper` in this PR compared with develop for accepted inputs, omitted-output allocation, output identity, default layouts, parameter order): the grouped-GEMM wrappers, the SDPA wrappers, `kimi_delta_attention` and the FROST compilers keep develop's torch-op behaviour; the one regression found (`kimi_delta_attention` refusing a fused-projection slice) is fixed in 24c5e77a3.

## Testing

Per-branch sweeps at 50aa22dfd (venv cuDNN 9.27, DSL 4.8 site, `-n 4`, `-m "L0 or L1"`):

| GPU | group | result |
|---|---|---|
| B200 | `gemm/frost/test_moe_grouped_matmul_fwd.py -k "workspace or reduction"` | 87 passed, 6 skipped |
| B200 | `fe_api/test_rule8_detector.py` + `test_grouped_gemm_rule8.py` + the `test_grouped_gemm_*bf16*` files | 29 passed |
| B200 | `fe_api/grouped_gemm` | 1606 passed, 1328 skipped; 1 failed = pre-existing `test_grouped_gemm_dsrelu_deterministic_at_scale` (1 of 16.7M elements, same on develop), 1 error = pre-existing JAX import |
| B200 | `sdpa/frost` ratchet / api_contract / fp8_sm100 / prepared_thd / fwd_dsl_sm100 | 932 passed, 6 skipped |
| H100 | `linear_attention/test_kda_sm90_{cuda,dsl}.py`, `test_hopper_marshal_stream.py` | 49 passed, 4 skipped; 2 failed = the two cuTile fall-through ids (no `tileiras` on this box, pre-existing) |
| H100 | `fe_api/test_rule8_detector.py` + `test_grouped_gemm_rule8.py` | 2 passed, 25 skipped |
| CPU | `test/python/api_index` (scanner over every module but `cudnn.jax`, no local jax) | index matches the package |

Hopper KDA host time per call on H100 (`kimi_delta_attention`, `kda_hopper_cuda`): 2048/12/1 112.1 → 108.2 µs, 4096/12/1 112.5 → 108.0 µs (median of 5×100, no kernel change).

The union of this branch and the five module branches reproduces the tree of the former stack top (3fe9c51e1 + develop merge 9b380b268) exactly.

## Summary by CodeRabbit

* **New Features**
  * Grouped GEMM, MoE, SDPA, and linear-attention class APIs support caller-provided scratch workspace and report required sizes; convenience wrappers allocate workspace automatically.
  * Workspace validation checks buffer size, alignment, device, and contiguity. Invalid workspace is rejected before execution can modify outputs.
  * Added WGrad expert-pointer utilities and optional device-value validation. Improved support for staged inputs and optional state handling; SM80 SDPA accepts a precomputed cosine/sine RoPE table.

* **Documentation**
  * Clarified workspace requirements, device-data contracts, validation options, and CUDA graph behavior.

* **Tests**
  * Expanded coverage of workspace validation, allocation-free execution, graph capture, synchronization, pointer handling, and staging.

Reviewed `50aa22dfd781` against base `9e9050c4013e`.

The ownership direction makes sense, but the high-risk eager-JAX workspace lifetime issue from #1174 remains and must be addressed before merging. All 78 changed files are byte-identical to the reviewed stack top 9b380b268, so I reused its relevant validation and reran the discriminating lifetime/device probes on this exact head. CodeRabbit's linked lifetime comment is confirmed on the unfused wrapper as well as the source-supported sibling paths: a pending consumer reads a later allocation's bytes. Retention must last until each launch completes; retaining only the most recent workspace until the next execute is insufficient when calls overlap. Use completion-aware ownership or framework-integrated launch lifetime. The separate CPU/wrong-device workspace guard below also remains. Prior review: https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1174#pullrequestreview-5274694451. The shared WorkspaceCarver reshape/copy concern remains a nonblocking follow-up from https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1172#pullrequestreview-5274566515.

- **[P1 · blocks approval] Still open:** [Keep eager JAX scratch alive until every consuming launch completes](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#discussion_r4069192313).

**[P2] Reject CPU or wrong-device workspace before extracting its raw pointer** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/50aa22dfd78147a865721f7ec23989b6e96bd3f0/python/cudnn/gemm/cutedsl/grouped/unfused/_bf16_api.py#L571))

The new caller-workspace path validates size/contiguity/alignment but not CUDA memory kind or agreement with the plan's device. An aligned CPU uint8 tensor therefore passes and its host address reaches the CUDA descriptor-workspace argument. A different GPU is likewise unchecked here. Add a shared metadata-only guard for the migrated APIs and cover CPU and other-device buffers alongside the existing missing/undersized/misaligned cases. This is the unresolved validation finding from the superseded #1174, carried here for the owning core PR.

Evidence: Exact-head discrete BF16 probe intercepts the compiled-kernel call: CPU workspace is accepted and the forwarded pointer equals the CPU tensor's data_ptr(). No GPU kernel is launched through that pointer. Wrong-device acceptance is source-supported rather than a cross-GPU launch reproduction. Attribution: New caller-workspace exposure; the generic Workspace helper's missing device guard predates this change. Unchanged since the #1174 review.

Validation: Exact-head SM100/JAX 0.11 / Torch 2.13 / DSL 4.7 / cuDNN 9.26: existing eager-JAX numerical comparison passed; pending-consumer probe reproduced early scratch reuse, and the retained-reference control passed. **Actionable comments posted: 1**

> [!CAUTION]
> Some comments are outside the diff and can’t be posted inline due to GitHub limitations.
> 
> **⚠️ Outside diff range comments (1)**
> 
> <details>
> <summary><em>🟡 Minor</em> · Validate that <code>workspace</code> is CUDA and on the execution device before carving. · <code>api_dsl.py:5689</code></summary><blockquote>
> 
> `python/cudnn/sdpa/fwd/api_dsl.py:5689`
> _🩺 Stability & Availability_ | _🟡 Minor_ | _⚡ Quick win_
> 
> **Validate that `workspace` is CUDA and on the execution device before carving.**
> 
> When `scratch_workspace_bytes()` is non-zero, `SdpaFwdDslSm80.execute()` constructs a `WorkspaceCarver` at line 5689. The `WorkspaceCarver` class validates size, contiguity, and dtype (api_base.py:1177–1182), but does not validate CUDA placement or device matching. The contract states (api_base.py:1165–1166): "The carver does not check CUDA placement or device index; this is a caller contract."
> 
> If `workspace` is a CPU `torch.Tensor` or resides on a different GPU, the carved tensors remain on that device. Operations like `dst.copy_(view)` at lines 5707 and later force unintended GPU-to-CPU or cross-GPU transfers. The CUDA kernel then receives host-backed or misplaced operands, leading to execution failures.
> 
> Add a device validation before carver construction. Alternatively, add the check to `WorkspaceCarver.__init__()` so all callers enforce the same contract.
> 
> <details>
> <summary>🐛 Suggested fix</summary>
> 
> ```diff
>          # Per-execute scratch is carved from the caller's workspace (R2; sized
>          # by scratch_workspace_bytes(), issue `#514`) in the sizing order. A chunk
>          # the sizing did not declare is a bug, never an allocation.
>          required = self.scratch_workspace_bytes()
> +        if required and (workspace is None or workspace.device != device or not workspace.is_cuda()):
> +            raise ValueError(f"cudnn.sdpa: SdpaFwdDslSm80 workspace must be CUDA and on the execution device {device}")
>          carver = WorkspaceCarver(workspace, required, "SdpaFwdDslSm80") if required else None
> ```
> 
> </details>
>

- yihuawei · COMMENTED · 2026-10-06T02:30:02Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#pullrequestreview-5423113409)

**Codex bot review · model: `gpt-6-astra`**

Reviewed `fd6f4ff6c867` against base `69e61cf0f3bd`.

The per-stream scratch reuse removes repeated wrapper allocation work, but the new cache aliases distinct per-thread default streams. Please address the high-risk P1 before merging, and resolve the already-known develop conflicts. Posting COMMENT and retaining the existing approval; this follow-up does not dismiss or replace prior reviews.

**[P1 · high risk] Keep per-thread default stream scratch separate across host threads** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/fd6f4ff6c86776a34f06956172b36efb96e4969a/python/cudnn/gemm/cutedsl/grouped/backend_utils.py#L164))

When two host threads call a grouped GEMM wrapper on the same device with current_stream=CUstream(2), this key is (device, 2) in both threads, and the second call returns the first thread's scratch. CU_STREAM_PER_THREAD denotes a different CUDA stream in each host thread; their GPU work is not serialized even if the host calls are issued one at a time. These wrappers pass the original handle through to the kernel, whose helper writes TMA descriptors and/or resets the scheduler counter in that scratch before the main kernel consumes it. A later call on the other thread can therefore overwrite in-flight descriptor/counter state. The old allocator gave these calls distinct buffers. Include the host-thread identity for the per-thread sentinel, resolve a true stream identity, or bypass reuse for this sentinel, and add a two-thread regression with outstanding work. This is high-risk because the alias can corrupt scheduling or memory accesses; please fix it before merging. CUDA's stream semantics are documented at https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-runtime-api/stream-sync-behavior.html.

Evidence: Executed the actual allocator helper from fd6f4ff6 with synthetic Torch/CUDA boundaries in two distinct, simultaneously alive host threads, serializing their host calls. Both calls using handle2 returned the identical Buffer object. The same experiment with predecessor445c40e5 returned distinct buffers. Ordinary explicit-stream/device separation, cache growth and capture bypass controls pass. Source trace: grouped/unfused/api.py forwards current_stream unchanged; _bf16_api.py passes it to the compiled kernel; unfused/moe_grouped_gemm.py helper_kernel writes workspace descriptors/counter and main launch consumes them. No GPU race or numerical corruption was executed on this CPU worker. Attribution: Introduced by this head's process-wide cache keyed only by device and raw stream handle; the predecessor allocates separate scratch per call.

Validation: Reviewed the entire 13-file increment since independently reviewed445c40e5, including all carve_workspace substitutions, cached scratch size, wrapper allocation and retention paths. Earlier full Rule8 and GPU validation remains attributed to the previous reviews; it is reused only for unchanged code. Ran actual-source host cache experiments against candidate and predecessor with synthetic allocation/capture APIs. Verified the per-thread-handle collision and ordinary-stream/device separation, capacity growth and capture bypass. All changed Python files parse and git diff --check passes. Checked live head, discussion, existing approval and merge status. The owner already reports unresolved develop conflicts. Appropriate existing labels and Frontend1.31.0 milestone are retained; project query remains unavailable with this token. No new CI was started.

Limitations: This worker has no GPU or CuTeDSL runtime. The reproduced defect is the host-side buffer identity collision; GPU corruption is a source/stream-semantics consequence, not an observed numerical failure. The author's 950 passing GPU cases and host-overhead figures are separately attributed and do not cover this two-thread stream identity case. The current branch conflicts with develop. This is not a review of the future merge resolution or a green CI qualification.

Comment only: this PR already has an active approval; preserving existing approvals.

Please make sure the high-risk P1 findings above are addressed before merging. Existing approvals are left unchanged.

## Walkthrough

This change moves scratch, state, and staging storage into caller-provided workspaces across grouped GEMM, MoE, SDPA, and Hopper linear-attention paths. APIs expose workspace sizes and validate buffers. Wrappers, benchmarks, documentation, and tests use the updated contracts.

### Changes

**Caller-owned workspace execution**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|---|---|
|**Workspace utilities and stream lifetime** <br> `python/cudnn/api_base.py`, `python/cudnn/gemm/cutedsl/grouped/backend_utils.py`, `python/cudnn/_torch_stream.py`|Added aligned workspace carving, wrapper allocation, asynchronous lifetime retention, and stream-aware tensor helpers. Grouped GEMM device-value checks can be enabled with an environment variable.|
|**Grouped GEMM runtime workspace** <br> `python/cudnn/gemm/cutedsl/grouped/*`, `python/cudnn/gemm/cutedsl/discrete_grouped/*`, `python/cudnn/gemm/cutedsl/grouped/wgrad/*`|Grouped GEMM APIs expose `scratch_workspace_bytes()`, accept workspace in `execute()`, and pass runtime workspace pointers to dense and discrete kernels. WGrad adds `wgrad_expert_ptrs` and updates discrete pointer-table handling.|
|**MoE launch and SDPA workspace paths** <br> `python/cudnn/gemm/frost/sm100/compiler.py`, `python/cudnn/gemm/frost/sm120/compiler.py`, `python/cudnn/sdpa/fwd/api_dsl.py`|MoE paths validate workspace before initializing reduction outputs. SDPA adapters carve scratch from caller workspace. The SM80 wrapper prepares RoPE tables before execution.|
|**Hopper linear-attention staging** <br> `python/cudnn/linear_attention/hopper/*`, `python/cudnn/linear_attention/ops/kda.py`|Hopper KDA plans use workspace for state, scratch, and declared staging. KDA inputs are made contiguous before graph binding.|
|**API guidance and validation** <br> `docs/fe-oss-apis/gemm_fusions/*`, `python/cudnn/AGENTS.md`, `test/python/*`, `benchmark/*`|Documentation and examples describe workspace and device-data contracts. Tests cover workspace validation, allocation and synchronization behavior, CUDA graph capture, pointer tables, staging, and MoE output preservation.|

- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-09-22T23:37:40Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#issuecomment-5786191037)

CI on the previous heads (pipelines 69337391 / 69343938 / 69344252 / 69344419 / 69344711 / 69344867 lineage): the only sm90 red in every rel/dev job was `test_causal_conv1d.py::test_causal_conv1d_compiled_autograd[nhw]` ('module' object is not callable) — root cause: `cudnn.ops` exports `causal_conv1d` lazily via `__getattr__`, and once an xdist worker has run a test that does `from cudnn.ops.causal_conv1d import ...` FIRST, the submodule object is bound at that name and `cudnn.ops.causal_conv1d(...)` calls a module. Pre-existing trap (develop passes by distribution luck; our extra fe_api tests shifted the worker split). Fixed on the test side in 31d39a3fa (import the functions from the module); the package-side fix (private submodule name, like `_causal_conv1d_update.py`) belongs to the ops owner. `analysis:guardwords_scan` = #750 moe_ep sources (red on develop too).

develop merged forward again (cd9c52295): develop a5d61f45c (#1209 mask-form collapse + FP8 suite Rubin cases, #794 torch-free conftest) conflicted in `sdpa/frost/test_sdpa_fwd_fp8_sm100.py` — this PR's new `test_fp8_thd_execute_requires_a_workspace` sits directly above `test_fp8_thd`, whose stacked parametrize #1209 collapsed into `_THD_CASES`; kept the new test, took develop's parametrization. B200 sdpa/frost (fp8_sm100, ratchet, api_contract, prepared_thd, fwd_dsl_sm100) + detectors on the merge: 942 passed, 14 skipped.

Also fixed in 28365cea8 (found while re-running the detectors on H100): under CUDA lazy module loading a kernel's first launch in a process waits for the device to drain, so when the poison fill or the staging copy had not run yet in that process the poison landed after the delayed copy — the negative control failed (the control alone on H100: 3/3) and the positive detectors passed without any race. Both helpers now launch every kernel of the timed window once first; AGENTS.md R1 records the trap next to the detector recipe. Verified on H100 and B200, each test in a fresh process: all pass, and the positive tests fail with `record_streams` patched to a no-op. The DSA / NSA / HSTU regressions got the same warm-up in their own PRs (each verified the same way).

- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-09-24T07:16:55Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#issuecomment-5809602793)

develop moved again right after the previous push: #1187 (FROST BF16 dGLU activation recompute) conflicted in 7 files (grouped-GEMM `backend_utils.py`, dGLU `api.py` / `_bf16_api.py` / `_blockscaled_api.py`, `AGENTS.md`, the dGLU doc and test). #1187 carried an earlier snapshot of part of this PR's grouped-GEMM contract into dGLU and added its own pieces, so the merge (223770d06) keeps both: #1187's activation recompute, GeGLU/clamp parameters, `Workspace(device=)`, `validate_workspace_aliases`, memoized scratch size, launch-stream reads in the debug validators and `wrapper_workspace` (stream-ordered eager-JAX scratch for the dGLU wrappers), plus this PR's `retain_workspace` after the carve. `retain_workspace` now accepts a `DeviceView` (it owns no memory: a `Workspace` carve or `wrapper_workspace`'s allocation) with a device check and no retention; without that, #1187's JAX dGLU path would have been refused as a non-CUDA buffer. New tests in 9267a3fd3 cover it (unit + an SM100 dGLU execute with a `DeviceView` workspace matching the torch-workspace result; both fail with the branch removed). `AGENTS.md` keeps #1187's JAX/device paragraph and this PR's R2 addenda, with the stale "held until the next execute" sentence now describing `retain_workspace`.

Local B200 on the merge: whole `fe_api/grouped_gemm` + Rule 8 detectors = 1771 passed; reds = the known dsrelu at-scale id, the JAX-module import error, and #1187's two JAX workspace tests, which fail at their first `jax` call because JAX is not installed on this box (CI has it). H100: causal_conv1d, import boundaries, buffer probe, KDA suites = 125 passed + the 2 known cuTile ids. Follow-up, not merge work: the other grouped-GEMM wrappers still allocate eager-JAX scratch with `allocate_wrapper_workspace` + `retain_workspace`; converging them on `wrapper_workspace` is a separate change.

Correction to my comments of 2026-09-23 ([1](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#issuecomment-5790837625), [2](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#issuecomment-5791945655)): cuDNN 9.28.0.13 does not fix NVBug 6813175. On SM107 its heuristic ranks the working engine 8 first, while native engines 10 and 18 still fail NVRTC. The 12 XPASS(strict) came from that ranking change; #1220 keys the marker on the selected engine.

Local on the merge:
- B200: SDPA FROST suites (Rule 8 ratchet, API contract, graph analyzer, prepared THD, fwd DSL SM100, prepared SM120), `test_sdpa_ragged_decode_stats` and the Rule 8 detectors: 879 passed, 4 failed. The 4 are `test_sdpa_ragged_decode_stats[decode_gqa_ragged-*]` (Stats mismatch on the engine 8 plan); they also fail on clean develop 57f663a45 with this box's cuDNN 9.27 prerelease, and #1220 reports them passing on B200 with 9.28.
- A100: SM80 SDPA frontend integration, stream respect and THD bwd: 83 passed.

CI not re-triggered yet: mirror image pulls from the GitLab registry still fail project-wide with `HTTP Basic: Access denied` (newest pipelines 69835250 and 69838385), so build jobs fail and every test lane is skipped. The previous pipelines on #1181–#1186 (69616059–69616609) are invalid for the same reason: 50 pull/auth job failures in total. Their only test-level reds are two known hecate sm107 worker crashes (`test_sdpa_random_bwd_L0[test448]` on #1182, `test_sdpa_random_bwd_ragged_L0[test375]` on #1185; `unspecified launch failure` plus torch cuBLAS `CUBLAS_STATUS_INTERNAL_ERROR`). - YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-09-30T20:33:16Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#issuecomment-5919245575)

Merged develop 347e186e6 (67 commits, including the FROST prepared-launch migration #1256–#1273 / #1290 and the test reorganization #1232) as bd77903da, with follow-ups through cfdc1fb09. Following the pre-merge audit, the SDPA code was ported onto develop's prepared paths instead of resolved hunk by hunk:

- `sdpa/fwd/api_dsl.py`: develop's file is the base. Ported: `WorkspaceCarver` / `ws_align` / `_WS_ALIGN` from `api_base`; the hardened `_scratch_base` (missing buffer, torch type, plan device, contiguity, alignment); no allocate-when-missing fallback in the SM100 dense split, SM100 THD (128-byte aligned for its TMA descriptors) or `_split_partials`; prepared FP8 validated through `_scratch_base`. SM120 and SM80 already require the workspace on develop. `scratch_workspace_bytes()` no longer reads `_prepared_fp8` / `_prepared_mxfp8` before a compile path sets them. The `_dummy` ratchet is pinned at 0.
- `sdpa/bwd`: develop's `api_dsl.py` as is; `prepared_sm100` requires a 128-byte aligned base for THD, whose workspace holds the per-sequence TMA descriptors.
- FROST MoE (sm100 / sm120): the caller workspace is validated before develop's reduction-output initialization.
- Detector: #1232 moved `fe_api/` into per-operation directories, and git placed this PR's detector conftest under `gemm/cutedsl` only. It is now `test/python/rule8_detector.py`, registered by the root conftest (torch imported lazily) and armed for every per-operation home of the former `fe_api` tree; `test_every_covered_directory_exists` fails when one of them is renamed away.

Host overhead came from per-call Python work, not from the checks. `as_torch_stream` caches the non-owning torch stream per (handle, device), `stream_context` returns a context manager directly, `launch_stream` / `device_context` give wrappers one scope, and `WorkspaceCarver` skips redundant views. With the module changes in #1182 / #1185, B200 against develop 347e186e6 (interleaved, warm, median µs per call):

| Wrapper | develop | previous heads | now |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| DSA Top-K bf16 (32, 1024) K=128, explicit stream / none | 20.2 / 17.9 | 40.3 / 35.4 | 25.0 / 24.1 |
| HSTU fwd+bwd T=128 H=2 D=64 causal, no stream / explicit | 138 / 198 | 196 / 281 | 108 / 122 |

The ~5 µs left on Top-K is the caller-workspace carve and its checks. GPU time is unchanged.

Local, `-m "L0 or L1"`: B200 SDPA prepared/staged fwd+bwd 2859 passed; `gemm/cutedsl` + core + MoE 3616 passed; DSA 453, NSA 41, HSTU 368, flex 66, GAB 450. H100 core 242, DSA 135, flex 56, GAB 361. A100 SM80 prepared/staged 631. The remaining reds are environment-only on this box and also fail on clean develop: 4 `test_sdpa_ragged_decode_stats[decode_gqa_ragged-*]` (local cuDNN 9.27 prerelease), 64 `test_gemm_swiglu.py` cases, 3 ids needing the missing `tileiras`, the dsrelu at-scale id and 2 JAX tests (no JAX here).

Not ported: develop's prepared SM80 path builds the RoPE angle table inside `execute()` on every call; this PR had moved it to the wrapper. Follow-up.

Re-running CI with the OSS lanes.

- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-10-01T11:19:54Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#issuecomment-5930254679)

Merged develop 160f322f5 (the 15 commits after 347e186e6) as 42994e48e. There was one conflict, in the SM120 MoE compiler: develop's #1312 moved the scheduler-counter zeroing into the template's host. The merge keeps that, and the caller workspace is still validated before the reduction-output initialization.

Two findings from #1159 (SM90 D512 prefill):

- **Fixed here, 8452be384.** Its SM90 `execute()` bound a cached `torch.zeros` dummy into the seq-lens slot the plan does not read. That is the one `self._dummy` caller this PR's R3 ratchet (0) rejects. Both slots are now `Optional`, with a `None` fake unless a role reads them, the same treatment `lse` and `sinks` already get.
- **Not fixed here: develop is red on its own.** On clean develop 160f322f5, SM90 THD `execute()` raises `AttributeError: 'SdpaFwdDslSm90' object has no attribute '_thd_declared_total'`. #1159 calls `_thd_capacity` / `_thd_declared_total`, and #1236 removed both. 13 THD tests in `sdpa/frost/test_sdpa_fwd_dsl_sm90.py` fail on an H100. With the two helpers restored locally (uncommitted), all 117 tests in the file pass on this branch, including the R3 change above. `python:dev:sm90` will stay red on every PR until develop fixes this.

Local runs at these heads:

| GPU | Suite | Result |
|---|---|---|
| A100 | SM80 SDPA and core | 291 passed |
| H100 | SM90 SDPA + ratchet | 105 passed; the 13 develop THD failures |
| H100 | KDA SM90 | 2 failures from the missing local `tileiras` (environment only) |
| B200 | SDPA prepared/THD/split/FP8 | 1315 passed, plus the ratchet that 8452be384 fixes |
| B200 | GEMM, incl. #1313's wgrad tensormap tests | 667 passed |

**Fixed on develop after these runs started:**

- `python:dev` sm80 and sm90 (and sm100 on #1185) fail only `test_gnn_mha_gat{,_v2}_uses_current_stream`. The side stream did not wait for its default-stream inputs. develop #1314 (e2bf967b1) adds `stream.wait_stream(...)` to both tests.

**Retried:**

- #1181 only, `python/sdpa/frost:cutlass-rel:sm100`: three xdist workers died without a Python fault, and the job hit the 80-minute cap. The same code passed this lane on the other five PRs (9115 passed each, 71–76 minutes), so I retried the job as 464530679. I also retried sm103 on #1181 as 464530829, to check whether lyris is back.

- There is no SM90 `sdpa/frost` lane, so CI cannot see #1159's SM90 THD regression (described in the merge note above).
- develop has since taken #1314 and #1207 (compact GQA backward, SM107). All six PRs are still MERGEABLE/CLEAN.
- #1207's new `sdpa/cutedsl` tests fall under this PR's sync detector. Their `execute` stages metadata through pinned host memory with `non_blocking` copies, and the tests that launch are L2. I don't expect them to trip the detector; the next trigger will confirm it.


- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-10-01T17:15:41Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#issuecomment-5936631592)

The shared P2 (the default-stream fast path must preserve the device context) is fixed in 16caa862d.

- `stream_context` now returns a no-op only when the requested device is current and any `torch.cuda.Stream` passed in lives on that device. In every other case, `torch.cuda.stream(...)` either switches the device or rejects the Stream/device pair, as before the fast path.
- New two-GPU tests in `core/torch/test_torch_stream.py` cover handles 0/1/2, default and side streams, following a Stream across devices, and the Stream/device mismatch. 6 of them fail on the previous helper.
- The current device is read through torch's raw getter. On B200, each enter and exit costs about 0.2 µs more with an explicit device and about 0.1 µs less without one.

Local runs at the new heads:

| GPU | Result |
|---|---|
| A100 | 298 passed |
| B200 | SDPA 1363, GEMM 899, DSA 464, NSA 186, HSTU 368, flex 66, GAB 583; all passed |
| H100 | DSA 164, flex 56, GAB 494 passed. Core is unchanged: the 13 SM90 THD tests that fail on develop, plus 2 tests that need a local `tileiras` |
| H100 + B200 | `core/torch`: 22 passed |

A trial merge with develop eabd48021 has no conflicts.

**[P2] Two more workspace-contract tests need to opt out of the autouse shim.**

The newly finished SM100 SDPA job **470591363** in pipeline **71743094** reports 98 failures, all accounted for by these two tests:

- `test_standalone_split_requires_caller_workspace_without_allocating`: both native/Python cases fail because `conftest.py:48` allocates a workspace inside the test harness, triggering the allocation sentinel.
- `test_paged_thd_split_capture_lengths_and_stats`: 96 cases reach `standalone(None)` and fail with `DID NOT RAISE`, because the same fixture supplies the missing workspace before the real adapter sees it.

Neither function currently has `@pytest.mark.no_workspace_shim`. Add the existing opt-out marker to these two contract tests and rerun their selections; preserve their negative assertions. The production missing-workspace guards are still present, so these traces identify a test-fixture integration problem, not evidence that production execution allocates scratch. The job otherwise passed 11,310 tests, with 4,710 skips.

Correction to my review's scope: the missing-workspace tests I checked had the opt-out, but these two additional tests did not. My earlier statement about the negative tests was too broad. This is in addition to the API-index P2 already reported; other pipeline lanes are still pending.

- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-10-05T22:26:23Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#issuecomment-6004416725)

Status at 22:30 UTC: the branch (`445c40e5f`) conflicts again after develop `9d409e85a`. Six files conflict, and most of the conflict comes from #1233 (merged 21:33 UTC). On develop, #1233 restructured the grouped GLU and quant execute paths around a **plan-owned** workspace: `compile()` does `self._workspace = torch.empty(...)`, and every launch path uses `cached_workspace_ptr`. Its new `execute(..., scheduler_counter_tensor=None)` also takes the signature slot where this branch adds `workspace`.

Resolving this means re-applying the R2 transformation to #1233's new code: drop the compile-time allocation and carve the caller workspace in each launch path. `workspace` would be appended after `scheduler_counter_tensor` to keep the public signature append-only. That goes beyond textual conflict resolution, so it was not done blindly; the attempted merge was aborted. The smaller conflicts (`unfused/_bf16_api.py`, `sdpa/fwd/api_dsl.py`, `api_index.txt`) are mechanical. Needs owner coordination with #1233.

- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-10-06T01:30:57Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#issuecomment-6007474293)

Host overhead follow-up in `fd6f4ff6c`.

**What changed:** the grouped GEMM wrappers now reuse one scratch buffer per (device, stream) instead of allocating, carving and retaining a workspace on every call. That per-call chain was +11–13 µs per call. Plans still own no device memory (R2). Same-stream launches are serialized and every kernel re-initializes the scratch it reads, so reuse is safe. Under stream capture the wrapper allocates a fresh buffer.

**Host enqueue on B200:** median of 300 calls, each issued on an idle stream; merge base `54a716b` vs this head, three rounds each.

| Wrapper | Base (µs) | This head (µs) |
|---|---|---|
| `grouped_gemm_wrapper_sm100` dense | 31.1–32.3 | 32.0–32.8 |
| `grouped_gemm_wrapper_sm100` discrete | 35.3–36.1 | 36.2–37.3 |
| `sdpa_fwd_wrapper_dsl_sm100` decode (s_q=1, kv=8192, GQA 8) | 52.4–55.4 | 38.7–42.8 |
| `sdpa_fwd_wrapper_dsl_sm100` prefill (s=512) | 50.9–51.6 | 37.2–37.7 |

Device time is unchanged: decode is one kernel, 171 µs per call, on both trees.

**Tests:** the grouped GEMM Rule 8 suites plus the bf16 / glu / quant / srelu / dsrelu / wgrad / dglu / discrete SwiGLU / wrapper-memo suites (L0+L1, SM100): 950 passed. One failure: `test_grouped_gemm_dsrelu_deterministic_at_scale`. It fails identically on develop `20a3c87b5` and on the merge base. The cause is an exact e4m3 rounding tie in the test reference, not this branch.

The branch still conflicts with develop after #1233; that merge is separate.

- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-10-06T18:08:24Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#issuecomment-6022449546)

**Native SM100 follow-up to [the per-thread-stream finding](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1181#pullrequestreview-5423113409): reproduced missing output stores on `fd6f4ff6c867`. Please address this high-risk P1 before merging.**

Two host threads use separate memoized BF16 grouped-GEMM configurations, dense weights, dynamic scheduling, `M=16384`, `K=4096`, two experts, and `N=512` / `768`. Both pass `current_stream=CUstream(2)`. Driver stream IDs confirm that these are **two different execution streams**. Inputs, compilation and serial reference outputs are warmed first. An event delays both consumers until their launches are queued; outputs are prefilled with NaNs to detect missing stores.

- **Unmodified current head:** fails on the first paired launch. One GEMM leaves **10,616,832 elements unwritten in each of C and D**. The cache has one `(device, 2)` buffer shared by both threads.
- **Diagnostic control changing only scratch-cache identity for sentinel 2 to include the calling thread:** **20/20 paired launches pass**, bitwise equal to the serial outputs. Both launches were queued before the event released them in every round. This is an experiment, not a submitted production fix or a qualification of thread-ID lifetime/reuse.
- **Attribution caveat:** the exact predecessor `445c40e5f4f4` also fails, on paired launch 11. Keeping its separately allocated workspaces alive until GPU completion makes **20/20 rounds pass** (44 distinct retained allocations). Thus the new permanent cache collision is real, but scratch lifetime for this sentinel already had a problem. Simply reverting the cache or bypassing it for handle 2 does **not** establish safety. The older allocation/retention path maps handle 2 to Torch's legacy default stream while the kernel still receives the per-thread stream; please address the actual consumer lifetime as well.

For contrast, the separate ordinary-explicit-stream control completed 300 paired launches without error. That does not cover PTDS. All measurements here used the real public wrapper and native SM100 kernels with exact-source imports; the predecessor and candidate have identical native build inputs. No new CI, approval or merge action was taken.

Codex bot review — model `gpt-6-astra`.

**Remove the per-execution JAX host synchronization from automatic pointer generation.**

For JAX discrete execution with `wgrad_ptrs` omitted, both wrapper paths call `wgrad_expert_ptrs` before `op.execute`. Its JAX branch calls `jax.block_until_ready`, so each such execution blocks the host before launch. Rule 8 provides no exception for this pointer-generation path. Reuse a caller-owned table, or generate and consume it without a host wait. Explicit `wgrad_ptrs` callers are unaffected.

**Use a device index different from the current device.**

`_Desc.device` uses `torch.cuda.current_device()`. If the current device is 7, the fake buffer also uses `cuda:7`, so `retain_workspace()` does not raise the expected `ValueError`. Use the next index for this metadata-only fake buffer. This does not require another GPU because the mismatch check runs before any device access.

<details>
<summary>🐛 Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
-        retain_workspace(api, _EagerBuffer(torch.device("cuda", 7)), stream)
+        retain_workspace(
+            api,
+            _EagerBuffer(torch.device("cuda", torch.cuda.current_device() + 1)),
+            stream,
+        )
```

</details>

Done in edc5c3c5c (#1181, merged forward): the pending cap is gone. An entry is released only when its own fence has completed; an entry whose fence could not be recorded is released once a later entry on the same stream has completed (stream order). Nothing is evicted while it may be in flight and nothing blocks the host; the list is bounded by what is genuinely pending. Test fences the last buffer with a sentinel before synchronising.

**Require 128-byte alignment for the SM100 THD prepared path.**

`_scratch_base` validates only 16-byte alignment. `bind_thd` then places the TMA descriptors at offsets from that base. A contiguous workspace slice can be 16-byte aligned but not 128-byte aligned, which can pass validation with misaligned descriptors and fail at launch. Add an alignment parameter and use `_WS_ALIGN` for the THD path.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
-    def _scratch_base(self, workspace, label: str, required: Optional[int] = None) -> int:
+    def _scratch_base(self, workspace, label: str, required: Optional[int] = None, *, align: int = 16) -> int:
...
-        if base % 16 != 0:
-            raise ValueError(f"cudnn.sdpa: {label} workspace must be at least 16-byte aligned; got data_ptr=0x{base:x}")
+        if base % align != 0:
+            raise ValueError(f"cudnn.sdpa: {label} workspace must be at least {align}-byte aligned; got data_ptr=0x{base:x}")
...
-        ws_ptr = self._scratch_base(workspace, "SdpaFwdDslSm100 (THD)", spec.scratch_bytes)
+        ws_ptr = self._scratch_base(workspace, "SdpaFwdDslSm100 (THD)", spec.scratch_bytes, align=_WS_ALIGN)
```

</details>

**Remove `RuntimeError` from the unsupported exception handler.**

The `check_support()` implementations raise `RuntimeError` for environment failures (`raise RuntimeError("CUDA is not available")` in `_blockscaled_api.py:324` and `raise RuntimeError(f"GroupedGemmWgrad requires SM100+ compute capability...")` in `_blockscaled_api.py:328`), not for configuration unsupported cases. These environmental failures should not skip the test; they should fail it. The `_bf16_api.py` implementation raises only `ValueError` for configuration violations. Other tests at lines 181, 410, and 701 catch only `(ValueError, NotImplementedError)`, establishing the correct exception contract. Keep line 1031 consistent with the broader pattern.

```diff
     try:
         supported = op.check_support()
-    except (ValueError, NotImplementedError, RuntimeError) as e:
+    except (ValueError, NotImplementedError) as e:
         pytest.skip(f"Unsupported testcase: {e}")
```

File: `python/cudnn/_torch_stream.py` · line None

_🎯 Functional Correctness_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Record `src` on the consumer stream.**

`dst.copy_(src)` reads `src` asynchronously on `stream`. This helper records only `dst`. If the last `src` reference is released after this call and its allocation stream differs from `stream`, the caching allocator can reuse `src` before the copy reads it. Record both tensors before the copy.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
-    record_streams((dst,), stream, device)
+    record_streams((src, dst), stream, device)
```
</details>

_🎯 Functional Correctness_ | _🟡 Minor_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Release `dst` in the copy-back lifetime test.**

`keep = dst` keeps the destination allocation live. The allocator cannot reuse that block, so this test passes even if `copy_into_on_stream` stops recording `dst`. Save the pointer, then delete the final tensor reference before allocating `other`.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>

```diff

+    dst_ptr = dst.data_ptr()
     del dst
     other = torch.empty(_ROWS, _COLS, dtype=torch.float32, device="cuda")
     other.fill_(-1.0)
     torch.cuda.synchronize()
-    assert torch.equal(keep, src) and other.data_ptr() != keep.data_ptr()
+    assert other.data_ptr() != dst_ptr
```
</details>
